# Benchmarks

Measured, not claimed. The block between the `bench:full` markers is generated by `python -m eval.bench --full` from `eval/results/<stamp>.json` (gitignored; the markdown next to it is a copy of this block). Re-run it on the demo laptop the day before the event and the block, the three chart tables and the slide-safe claims refresh in place.

```
.venv/bin/python -m eval.make_fixtures --full          # 96 LibriSpeech two-talker + noise mixtures (network once)
.venv/bin/python -m eval.make_fixtures --av --full     # 160 GRID audio-visual mixtures with video (network once)
.venv/bin/python -m eval.dnsmos --download             # DNSMOS models (1.4 MB)
.venv/bin/python -m eval.bench --full                  # every mode x tier -> eval/results/<stamp>.{json,md} + this file
.venv/bin/python -m eval.bench --full --set tiny --av-set av_tiny --limit 4   # 2-minute smoke version
```

What `--full` runs (definitions in `eval/bench.py`, `full_bank()` / `slider_bank()`):

- **Natural** tiers 4 → 2: `dfn_ll` (default), `dfn`, `gtcrn` on the **noise-only** input (the same fixtures without the competing talker, which is what a denoiser is judged on, per PLAN) and on the full mix; `dfn_ll>gate` with an oracle-locked target; `remote(fallback='dfn_ll')` with the GPU box down; the Enrolled TSE tier.
- **Focus HQ**: windowed TSE through `hearing.tse.SlidingWindowExtractor` (hop = window / 2): `av_mossformer2` and `dolphin` on the AV set (lip crops from `video.mp4` at the manifest's target face), `blind_assoc` on both sets, and the clean-target `oracle` ceiling. At s < 1 the extracted target is put on the mix's scale and mixed as `target + (1 - s) * residual`.
- **Text**: captions of `synth(asr='local', speak=false)` on the cleaned and raw stream, scored by WER. **Synthetic**: phrase latency of the synth stage per TTS engine (mock, Kokoro, ElevenLabs when a key is set) and TTS intelligibility (WER of the reference transcriber on TTS renderings). **Quiet**: `silence`.
- Every streaming row is 10 ms chunks through `run_stream`, latency-compensated by the pipeline's declared `latency_ms`; `lag err` checks that declaration against the output (cross-correlation), so a stage that lies about its latency shows up in the table.
- Metrics: SI-SDRi, PESQ (wide-band), STOI, WER (faster-whisper `small.en`, first 32 fixtures of each set), DNSMOS P.835 SIG/BAK/OVRL + P.808, algorithmic latency, compute p50/p95 per hop, RTF, device. Real phone clips under `eval/fixtures/real/` get a DNSMOS table (no reference needed).

Caveats that every number below carries: gate and `blind_assoc` rows use an **oracle** speaking detector (clean-target energy; Light-ASD is not wired into the bench yet); Enrolled enrolls on 3 s of the clean target; compute times were measured on laptop-a while other swarm workers were running (load average ≈ 5), so they are upper bounds; the synthetic fixtures are not the venue room, which is why the real-clip DNSMOS table exists.

Run 2026-10-05T06:34:25-04:00 on `Aryans-MacBook-Pro.local` (Apple M4 Pro, torch device `mps`), git `b5c5fa532f34b9632420b2db4e626f0c832a0a73`. Main set `full` (32 fixtures, TSE tiers on 16); AV set `av_full` (32 fixtures). Bounded run: `--limit 32` / `--tse-limit 16` (the first fixtures by id; ids cycle SNR fastest, then SIR/layout, then noise type). WER by `whisper` (first 16 fixtures of each set; captions on all), DNSMOS by `dnsmos`, wall 21.4 min. JSON: `eval/results/20261005-063425.json`.

### Every mode and tier

Means over fixtures. `input`: `mix` = target + competing talker + noise; `noise only` = the same fixtures without the talker (what a denoiser is judged on); `AV` = the GRID audio-visual set (lip crops from the video). `algo ms` = algorithmic latency (a TSE window for Focus HQ / Enrolled). `p50/p95` = measured compute per 10 ms chunk (per window for TSE rows, marked `/win`). WER by the reference transcriber on the output (captions for Text). DNSMOS = P.835 OVRL / BAK. `lag err` = median residual lag of the output after the declared-latency shift (0 when `latency_ms` is honest). Gate rows and `blind_assoc` use an **oracle** speaking detector from the clean target; Enrolled enrolls on 3 s of the clean target.

### Slide-safe one-line claims

Generated from this run; each carries its condition. Do not drop the condition on the slide.

- Natural fast path (DeepFilterNet3-LL, 10 ms hops): 13 ms algorithmic latency + 0.7 ms p95 compute per 10 ms chunk (RTF 0.06) on an Apple M4 Pro laptop (Aryans-MacBook-Pro.local).
- At full suppression it improves SI-SDR by 4.1 dB on 32 LibriSpeech clips with babble/pink noise at -5 to 10 dB SNR (noise only, no competing talker).
- It lifts the DNSMOS background-noise score from 1.62 to 3.09 (1-5 scale) on the same clips.
- Focus HQ (`av_mossformer2`, 2 s windows) improves SI-SDR by 13.8 dB and cuts WER from 1.41 to 0.78 (above 1 on the raw mix: Whisper also transcribes the competing talker) on 16 GRID audio-visual two-talker + babble clips, ~2.5 s behind live on an Apple M4 Pro laptop (Aryans-MacBook-Pro.local) (given lip video of the target).
- Focus HQ (`blind_assoc`, 2 s windows) improves SI-SDR by 13.7 dB and cuts WER from 0.55 to 0.17 on 16 LibriSpeech two-talker + noise mixtures, ~2.4 s behind live on an Apple M4 Pro laptop (Aryans-MacBook-Pro.local) (given a perfect speaking signal).
- Enrolled tier (audio only, 3 s of the target's voice): +11.8 dB SI-SDRi with 2 s windows on 16 LibriSpeech two-talker + noise mixtures (clean enrollment).
- Text mode: live captions from local faster-whisper reach WER 0.59 from the raw mix vs 0.73 from the DeepFilterNet-cleaned stream on 32 LibriSpeech two-talker + noise mixtures, so ASR is fed the raw audio.
- Synthetic mode (kokoro TTS on an Apple M4 Pro laptop (Aryans-MacBook-Pro.local)): the re-voiced phrase starts 1695 ms (p50; p95 1878 ms) after the speaker stops, including a 400 ms end-of-phrase pause.

### Slide-safe claims for the AirPods demo path (T-148)

Hand-written. The demo plays **MacBook Pro Microphone (built-in) -> engine -> AirPods Pro 3 (Bluetooth A2DP)** on laptop-a
(Apple M4 Pro, local Mac ladder). Every line names capture device, backend and output device, and is tagged:
**engine-side** = measured inside the engine on recorded clips (no microphone, no Bluetooth: it excludes the ~150-250 ms
Bluetooth adds); **mouth-to-ear (AirPods, measured)** = clicks through the real devices, `bash scripts/bt_latency.sh`
(T-144, `results/bt_latency_<stamp>.json`). Never put an engine-side number on a slide as "latency" without the tag.

Engine-side (measured, source rows above):
- *engine-side* · capture: LibriSpeech + noise fixture clips (offline) · backend: `dfn_ll` on laptop-a (M4 Pro, local) · output: none (scored offline): Natural adds 13 ms algorithmic latency + 0.7 ms p95 compute per 10 ms chunk (RTF 0.06). (Slide-safe one-line claims, bench `--full`.)
- *engine-side* · capture: the `demo-mf-easy` replay clip · backend: Focus HQ, judge profile, Mac ladder on laptop-a (MPS) · output: none (`out_pre_agc.wav` scored): +8.3 to +8.5 dB separation SI-SDRi, other talker 18.1-18.8 dB down, lock -> Isolated 5.5-5.9 s (default hop rows of T-135).
- *engine-side* · capture: the `demo-mf-easy` replay clip · backend: Focus HQ Enrolled tier, Balanced preset, laptop-a (MPS) · output: none: plays 1.5 s behind live by design (window + hop, T-065 table; Speed 0.75 s, Accuracy 2.25 s).
- *device round trip, not the demo path* · capture: built-in mic · backend: passthrough (no model), laptop-a · output: built-in speakers: 64 ms at 16 kHz / 5 ms blocks (T-013 row). Not quotable for the AirPods.

Mouth-to-ear (AirPods, measured) — copy from the rehearsal sheet (DEMO.md §B, `bt_latency` JSON); until then these lines stay off the slides:
- *mouth-to-ear (AirPods, measured)* · capture: MacBook Pro Microphone · backend: `hearing latency-test --pipeline dfn_ll` on laptop-a · output: AirPods Pro 3 (A2DP): median ____ ms, p95 ____ ms (bar: median <= 300, p95 <= 350). Not measured yet: needs the AirPods on laptop-a and a human.
- *mouth-to-ear (AirPods, measured)* · capture: MacBook Pro Microphone · backend: `/judge` in Chrome, Natural, laptop-a · output: AirPods Pro 3 (A2DP): the console's mouth-to-ear number ____ ms. Not measured yet: read at the rehearsal.

### Reading the Oct 5 run (hand-written; re-check after a re-run)

- **Natural fast path is honest and cheap**: `dfn_ll` declares 13 ms and the lag check measures +0 ms; 0.7 ms p95 per 10 ms hop. On noise only it is the best denoiser here (+4.1 dB SI-SDRi, STOI 0.75 → 0.81, DNSMOS BAK 1.62 → 3.09); `dfn` is close (+4.0 dB, higher BAK 3.51) at 33 ms; `gtcrn` trails (+2.8 dB). With a competing talker a denoiser only gets +1.7 dB, and the oracle gate adds ~0.15-0.3 dB: the talker needs Focus HQ / Enrolled.
- **`remote(fallback='dfn_ll')` row is an alignment artefact, not a quality number**: with the GPU box down the stage plays its local fallback undelayed (by design, `hearing/server/remote.py`) but still declares `latency_ms` = the 200 ms playout buffer, so the bench shifts the output 187 ms too far (lag err −187) and SI-SDR collapses (−48 dB). Live it sounds like `dfn_ll`. It matters for the `tiers` ladder, which aligns tiers by declared latency (reported to T-023).
- **Focus HQ window length matters a lot**: AV-MossFormer2 gives +13.8 dB SI-SDRi at 2 s windows but +5.3 dB at 1 s (STOI 0.71 vs 0.50, below the input's 0.51), on the same 16 GRID clips. Superseded by the T-056 bake-off v2 below (80 two-face clips): `config/tiers.yaml` now runs AV-MossFormer2 only at 2 s, as the cold-start tier, and Enrolled at 1 s (Mac) / 0.5 s (GPU) as Tier 1.
- **Dolphin and blind_assoc fail on the AV set** (−3.7 / −4.0 dB): the AV set is GRID with babble at −5..10 dB SNR and two talkers whose lips we crop from video; `blind_assoc` picks a stream with the oracle speaking signal and still loses, so the likely cause is MossFormer2 separation on short (3 s) GRID clips; not investigated. On LibriSpeech mixes `blind_assoc` (+13.7 dB) and `enrolled` (+11.8 dB) work.
- **Captions are better from the raw mix** (WER 0.59) than from the `dfn_ll`-cleaned stream (0.73): feed ASR the raw audio (the server's default `SYNTH_BASE = "passthrough"`; docs/DEMO.md §4 names Synthetic/Text explicitly for that reason). WER on the mixes is high because Whisper also transcribes the competing talker.
- **Synthetic with Kokoro**: 1.7 s p50 from end of phrase to first re-voiced sample (400 ms endpoint pause + 1.3 s Kokoro time to first audio for a whole phrase); ElevenLabs was not measured (no key on this machine).
- **Real phone clips**: none recorded yet, so there is no real-room DNSMOS row; docs/DEMO.md §9 says how to add them.

## T-056 bake-off v2: Mac vs GPU compute per TSE window (2026-10-05)

Fresh `eval.bakeoff` runs on GRID two-face (`--set av_full --layout two_face --windows 0.5,1,2`, 80 clips of 3 s, `--enroll self`, hop = window / 2). Decision and quality tables: [BAKEOFF.md](BAKEOFF.md). Compute is wall time per window (`extract` on one window, including the GPU lock wait); `p95 headroom` = hop − p95 compute, so a negative value means the threaded stage falls behind at that hop. `MB` is the device memory the run reported (on MPS it is the process's allocation, which includes every model already loaded, so it is not comparable with CUDA's per-candidate peak). SI-SDRi is repeated to show the two hosts agree on quality. Mac compute was measured with other swarm workers running (load average 9-12): an upper bound. JSON: `$HEARING_RESULTS_DIR/bakeoff-mac.json/bakeoff_20261005-161323.json` (laptop-a) and `$HEARING_RESULTS_DIR/ilab/bakeoff-gpu-{A,C,D}.json/*.json` (iLab; `rlab4` did not log its GPU model). WER is not in this table: the GPU runs used the mock transcriber (0.000, not a measurement); the Mac's Whisper WER is in BAKEOFF.md.

Reading it: on the GPU every candidate keeps up at every window with ≥ 96 ms of p95 headroom. On the Mac, enrolled keeps up at 1 s / 0.5 s (+177 ms) but only just at 0.5 s / 0.25 s (+16 ms) and not at 2 s / 1 s (p95 1069 ms > 1 s hop, a long tail under load; p50 431 ms). AV-MossFormer2 at 2 s keeps up on both (Mac +502 ms). Hence `config/tiers.yaml`: enrolled 1 s / 0.5 s locally, 0.5 s / 0.25 s on the GPU backend, AV-MossFormer2 2 s / 1 s on either.

## Attention presets (T-067)
`python -m eval.target_eval --presets` on `eval/scenes` (accuracy per scene, 1 s grace after each expected switch; host `Aryans-MacBook-Pro.local`, CPU only). `g_look_down`: the target's face is lost for 1.5 s while the other person keeps talking; the target should not change.

| preset | dwell_s | hold_s | margin | a_turn_to_b | b_talk_over | c_occlusion | d_moving_target | e_silence_then_b | f_three_people | g_look_down | all | false switches |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| glance | 0.3 | 1.0 | 0.02 | 0.983 | 0.985 | 0.985 | 0.985 | 1.000 | 0.988 | 0.875 | 0.972 | 2 |
| normal | 0.5 | 2.0 | 0.04 | 0.983 | 0.985 | 0.985 | 0.985 | 1.000 | 0.988 | 0.985 | 0.987 | 0 |
| sticky | 1.0 | 4.0 | 0.1 | 0.931 | 0.985 | 0.985 | 0.985 | 1.000 | 0.828 | 0.985 | 0.957 | 0 |

Glance drops the target during the 1.5 s look-down (hold 1.0 s < 1.5 s); Sticky holds it but is slow to follow a real turn (`a_turn_to_b`, `f_three_people`). Normal is the default.

## T-061 face ↔ voice association, live-style auto-enrollment (2026-10-06)

Enrolled tier (`tse(candidate="enrolled", window_s=1.0, hop_s=0.5)`) driven end to end on 10 two-face GRID scenes × 2 speaker/side variants (s1 M / s4 F), enrollment harvested from the mix by Light-ASD through the session's `VisionEngine`. `baseline` = enrollment and matching before T-061; `final` = the new defaults (clean enrollment, margin 0.1, 2 s negative enrollment, 2 s enrollment, `OTHER_P` 0.7, sticky). Both include the separator level fix (streams were ≈ 30-36 dB above the input). Acc = share of extracted 1 s windows with the target talking whose output is closer to the target than to the other talker (or is the target's stream when the other is silent); wrong = seconds of other-talker-dominated output per 10 s of extracted output. Decision, definitions and ablations: [decisions/association.md](decisions/association.md). Host Aryans-MacBook-Pro.local (laptop-a M4 Pro, torch mps). JSON: `$HEARING_RESULTS_DIR/association_20261006-073002.json`.

| condition | config | acc (pooled / worst scene) | wrong s / 10 s (pooled / worst) | targets locked | lock s mean / worst | re-acquired after occlusion / away / exit | enrollment purity mean / worst | other person played (windows) | verdict (acc ≥ 0.95 and wrong < 0.5) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| SNR 5 babble | baseline | 0.906 / 0.78 | 0.39 / 1.36 | 6 / 22 | 8.3 / 10.5 | 0 / 6 | 0.95 / 0.90 | 4 / 83 | NO-GO |
| SNR 5 babble | final | 0.945 / 0.70 | 0.20 / 0.87 | 19 / 22 | 3.7 / 10.0 | 6 / 6 (1.0 s) | 0.96 / 0.34 | 12 / 364 | NO-GO |
| SNR 0 babble | baseline | 0.812 / 0.57 | 0.51 / 2.27 | 6 / 22 | 8.3 / 10.5 | 0 / 6 | 0.94 / 0.89 | | NO-GO |
| SNR 0 babble | final | 0.875 / 0.56 | 0.37 / 1.38 | 19 / 22 | 3.7 / 9.5 | 6 / 6 (1.0 s) | 0.96 / 0.28 | | NO-GO |
| SNR 10 pink | baseline | 0.983 / 0.92 | 0.80 / 5.16 | 6 / 22 | 8.9 / 12.5 | 0 / 6 | 0.94 / 0.89 | | NO-GO |
| SNR 10 pink | final | 0.980 / 0.84 | 0.35 / 1.47 | 19 / 22 | 3.8 / 9.5 | 6 / 6 (1.2 s) | 0.96 / 0.28 | | GO |

Two of the 22 targets can never lock (the `b_speaks_a_silent` target never talks). Wall time on laptop-a: about 6 min for all 20 scenes with two configs (vision ≈ 13-17 s per 12-16 s scene, TSE ≈ 2-5 s), about 2 min for any number of configs with `--vision-cache`, and `--fast` (5 scenes) below.

`--fast` subset (5 scenes: overlap, target_pauses, occlusion, mouth_no_sound, switch; variant ab; SNR 5 babble; baseline + final), re-run after the rebase onto main: wall **104 s** on laptop-a (mps), JSON `association_20261006-073536.json`.

| config | acc pooled / worst | wrong s / 10 s pooled / worst | targets locked | reacq | purity mean / worst | verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| baseline | 0.923 / 0.92 | 0.75 / 1.07 | 1 / 6 | 0 / 1 | 0.89 / 0.85 | NO-GO |
| final | 0.818 / 0.10 | 0.51 / 0.99 | 6 / 6 | 1 / 1 (1.0 s) | 0.86 / 0.32 | NO-GO |

The subset is weighted toward the hard cases (overlap, `mouth_no_sound`), so it reads worse than the 20-scene run: `final`'s pooled accuracy here is pulled down by `mouth_no_sound` (0.10, 0 s wrong-target audio, purity 0.32), and baseline's 0.923 is over the 13 windows of the only target it locked.

2026-10-06: real cached `dfn_ll>gate>mixer`, local WebSocket, 20 ms PCM / 25 fps
JPEG. MediaPipe is absent on this worker: **audio-only fallback**, no claim about
real-face detection or target separation. No microphone or speakers opened.

faces, target selection, gate and audio output through the same session and tap.

## T-069 association: pause handling and same-gender pairs (2026-10-06)

Same eval as T-061 (`eval/association_eval.py`, 10 scenes × 2 variants, Enrolled tier window 1 s / hop 0.5 s, SNR 5 dB babble) on three GRID pairs: `mf` s1/s4, `mm` s1/s2, `ff` s4/s7. Pause variants = `final` + one rule for windows where no stream reaches cos 0.25 (`hold` the previous stream; `_asd` only if the target's ASD < 0.5; `duck` = silence). Ceiling = share of scored windows where some separated stream would have been correct. Decision and analysis: [decisions/association.md](decisions/association.md#addendum-t-069-2026-10-06-pause-handling-and-same-gender-pairs). Host Aryans-MacBook-Pro.local (laptop-a M4 Pro, torch mps). ## T-065 enrolled window curve (2026-10-06)

The added windows are 0.75, 1.25 and 1.5 s, with hop = window / 2. The Mac run uses
`MacBook-Air.local` (laptop-b, arm64, CPU, Python 3.14.7, torch 2.14.1) and the first
20 GRID `two_face` clips (60 s of audio); all six windows are rerun on that same subset.
This is a different host and smaller subset than T-056's 80-clip M4 Pro/MPS run above.
Enrollment is `--enroll self`: the clean target's entire 3 s clip, an oracle enrollment
condition, not live auto-enrollment. The mock transcriber is used solely to omit costly ASR;
its zero WER is not a measured WER. Separation and ECAPA use the cached real weights.

Reproduce on a host with the fixture bank and weights (set `HEARING_MODELS_DIR` to its
shared model cache and `HEARING_RESULTS_DIR` to its persistent results directory):

```sh
.venv/bin/python -m eval.bakeoff --set /path/to/generated/av_full --layout two_face \
  --candidates enrolled --windows 0.5,0.75,1,1.25,1.5,2 --limit 20 \
  --enroll self --transcriber mock --out "$HEARING_RESULTS_DIR/t065"
```

**Delay interpretation:** threaded TSE plays **window + hop** behind capture, not just
window (the offline bake-off's `algorithmic_latency_ms` field only counts window).
The delay column below is that configured software playout delay. It excludes device buffers,
network/jitter buffers and scheduling overruns; it is not a physical microphone-to-headphone
loopback measurement. Compute p50/p95 is measured per extraction window. Headroom is hop minus
p95 compute; negative headroom means this host cannot reliably serve that streaming hop.

**GPU status: measured by T-075 (2026-10-06)** on the iLab A100, 8 clips, 5 windows: see the
"T-065 scoped curve" table below (A100 rows). T-056's existing CUDA rows (80 clips, RTX 4500 Ada)
are retained above.

**Association (wrong-target duration per window):** `eval/association_eval.py --fast --configs final`
(5 hard scenes of the T-061 set, variant ab, M/F pair, SNR 5 dB babble, M4 Pro mps, hop = window / 2), laptop-a, 2026-10-06:

| window s | pooled acc | wrong-target s / 10 s | wrong-person windows | targets locked | verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0.5 | 0.764 | 0.80 | 19 / 171 | 6 / 6 | NO-GO |
| 0.75 | 0.844 | 0.43 | 6 / 112 | 6 / 6 | NO-GO |
| 1.0 | 0.818 | 0.51 | 7 / 88 | 6 / 6 | NO-GO |
| 1.25 | 0.850 | 0.09 | 1 / 68 | 5 / 6 | NO-GO |
| 1.5 | 0.880 | 0.00 | 0 / 56 | 5 / 6 | NO-GO |
| 2.0 | 0.872 | 0.00 | 0 / 44 | 5 / 6 | NO-GO |

Wrong-target time falls steadily with window length (0.80 s / 10 s at 0.5 s to 0 at 1.5 s), which supports the
preset ladder: Speed (0.5 / 0.25) trades identity errors for delay, Accuracy (1.5 / 0.75) removes them in this subset.
Small subset (6 targets), so treat the trend, not the decimals; one target stops locking at 1.25 s and above.

The preset mapping remains Speed = 0.5/0.25 s, Balanced = 1/0.5 s, Accuracy = 1.5/0.75 s.
SI-SDRi is flat across windows and the association data above favours longer windows, so the mapping
stands; only the CUDA midpoints (0.75/1.25/1.5 s) are still unmeasured. The labels do
not promise that longer windows improve live speaker identity. The CPU live ladder still
requires a GPU for enrolled extraction and falls back normally when none is available.

Raw evidence: the Mac CPU bake-off JSON (not committed; it lived in `$HEARING_RESULTS_DIR/t065`), run
`20261006-022518`; elapsed 2106.5 s (35.1 min). Fast verification ran concurrently
for three roughly 45 s intervals; these compute timings describe this session rather than
an idle-host guarantee. Every Mac row completed 20/20 fixtures without an extraction error.

| Host / device | Clips | Window / hop s | SI-SDRi dB | Compute p50 / p95 ms | RTF | Window + hop ms | p95 headroom ms | Wrong-target duration |
|---|---:|---|---:|---|---:|---:|---:|---|
| MacBook Air / CPU | 20 | 0.5 / 0.25 | +9.02 | 2402 / 2974 | 9.109 | 750 | -2724 | unavailable (runner not merged) |
| MacBook Air / CPU | 20 | 0.75 / 0.375 | +10.75 | 2842 / 2884 | 6.651 | 1125 | -2509 | unavailable (runner not merged) |
| MacBook Air / CPU | 20 | 1 / 0.5 | +8.93 | 3311 / 3392 | 5.544 | 1500 | -2892 | unavailable (runner not merged) |
| MacBook Air / CPU | 20 | 1.25 / 0.625 | +9.91 | 3981 / 4150 | 5.345 | 1875 | -3525 | unavailable (runner not merged) |
| MacBook Air / CPU | 20 | 1.5 / 0.75 | +9.81 | 4566 / 5294 | 4.677 | 2250 | -4544 | unavailable (runner not merged) |
| MacBook Air / CPU | 20 | 2 / 1 | +9.38 | 5585 / 5708 | 3.734 | 3000 | -4708 | unavailable (runner not merged) |
| iLab RTX A4500 / CUDA (T-056) | 80 | 0.5 / 0.25 | +10.08 | 109 / 154 | 0.418 | 750 | +96 | unavailable |
| iLab GPU (blocked) | — | 0.75 / 0.375 | unmeasured | unmeasured | — | 1125 (configured) | — | unavailable |
| iLab RTX A4500 / CUDA (T-056) | 80 | 1 / 0.5 | +10.84 | 123 / 175 | 0.216 | 1500 | +325 | unavailable |
| iLab GPU (blocked) | — | 1.25 / 0.625 | unmeasured | unmeasured | — | 1875 (configured) | — | unavailable |
| iLab GPU (blocked) | — | 1.5 / 0.75 | unmeasured | unmeasured | — | 2250 (configured) | — | unavailable |
| iLab RTX A4500 / CUDA (T-056) | 80 | 2 / 1 | +11.46 | 191 / 218 | 0.130 | 3000 | +782 | unavailable |

The CUDA endpoints above are **historical T-056 measurements**, run `20261005-153514`,
copied from the earlier table; they are not new T-065 runs and use 80 rather than 20 clips.
The new CPU subset is **not monotonic**: 0.75 s gives the highest SI-SDRi (+10.75 dB),
versus +8.93 dB at Balanced (1 s) and +9.81 dB at Accuracy (1.5 s). Thus the current
preset names are provisional; this run does not establish that Accuracy improves quality.
All CPU RTFs exceed 1 and all p95 headrooms are negative, so none of these windows can
sustain real-time enrolled extraction on this CPU. The window + hop values are not achievable
mic-to-ear guarantees on this host. Confirm the GPU midpoints and association results before
finalizing the preset mapping or making a live quality/latency claim.

`eval.bakeoff --partial` (new) writes `bakeoff_<window>.json` as each window finishes, so a cut-off

Python 3.12.8, torch 2.14.1), run `20261006-033740`, 43 s, 8/8 clips per window, no errors. Raw JSON: not committed (results live in `$HEARING_RESULTS_DIR`); the table below is its 10-line summary.
Same oracle self-enrollment caveat as above; delay = window + hop, configured playout delay only.

| Host / device | Clips | Window / hop s | SI-SDRi dB | Compute p50 / p95 ms | RTF | Window + hop ms | p95 headroom ms | Wrong-target duration |
|---|---:|---|---:|---|---:|---:|---:|---|
| M4 Pro / MPS | 8 | 0.5 / 0.25 | +7.28 | 137 / 151 | 0.544 | 750 | +99 | unavailable (runner not merged) |
| M4 Pro / MPS | 8 | 0.75 / 0.375 | +8.04 | 186 / 221 | 0.450 | 1125 | +154 | unavailable |
| M4 Pro / MPS | 8 | 1 / 0.5 | +4.40 | 228 / 240 | 0.386 | 1500 | +260 | unavailable |
| M4 Pro / MPS | 8 | 1.5 / 0.75 | +5.14 | 319 / 348 | 0.323 | 2250 | +402 | unavailable |

| iLab A100-PCIE-40GB / CUDA (T-075) | 8 | 0.75 / 0.375 | +7.75 | 68 / 174 | 0.195 | 1125 | +201 | |

| iLab A100-PCIE-40GB / CUDA (T-075) | 8 | 1.25 / 0.625 | +6.73 | 75 / 182 | 0.135 | 1875 | +443 | |
| iLab A100-PCIE-40GB / CUDA (T-075) | 8 | 1.5 / 0.75 | +5.30 | 77 / 184 | 0.112 | 2250 | +566 | |

Eight clips is a small, hard subset (the first two_face clips, low SNR), so absolute SI-SDRi is
lower than T-056's 80-clip numbers and the 1 s dip is clip-level noise, not a trend: the curve is
flat-to-noisy, as in T-056. It does not separate the windows on quality; all four keep up on MPS
(RTF < 1, positive headroom). Preset mapping stays Speed 0.5/0.25, Balanced 1/0.5, Accuracy
1.5/0.75: the choice is a delay/stability trade (longer windows give the extractor more context
and a lower RTF) rather than a measured quality win.

GPU column (T-075, 2026-10-06): iLab `rlab2`, NVIDIA A100-PCIE-40GB, job 432518 (the server's
allocation, bake-off run inside it with `srun --overlap --jobid=432518`), torch 2.8.0+cu128, run
`20261006-183957`, 8/8 clips per window, no errors, GPU memory 677 MB. Command, in `~/hearing-remote`:
`.venv/bin/python -m eval.bakeoff --set eval/fixtures/generated/av_full --layout two_face --candidates enrolled
--windows 0.5,0.75,1,1.25,1.5 --limit 8 --enroll self --transcriber mock --partial --out eval/results/t075`.
The A100 is 2x faster at p50 than MPS (68-77 ms vs 137-319 ms) and every window keeps up (RTF 0.11-0.31,
positive p95 headroom from +76 ms at 0.5 s). Its p95 is flat at ~180 ms (the 2-3 slowest windows per
clip, on a node shared with other jobs), so at 0.5 s / 0.25 s the headroom is the thinnest. Quality is
the same flat-to-noisy curve as on the Mac (the 1 s dip again, here +2.91 dB): the GPU does not change
the preset mapping. The iLab `av_full` set was generated on iLab, so the 8 clips match the Mac's by
name and recipe, not byte for byte (the 0.5 s row differs by 0.8 dB). Raw JSON stays on iLab
(`~/hearing-remote/eval/results/t075/`), not committed.

`HEARING_SESSION_LOG`, then `scripts/replay.py eval/fixtures/demo/grid-two-face.mp4 --mode focus_hq
--suppression 100` (12 s, two GRID faces, overlap 0.58); numbers from `scripts/session_report.py` on
the taps (main checkout `results/sessions/T-072-*`). "Before" = main at fc52a7f/5eb3b44 (AV tier
built on MPS, ASD waits 100 ms for the GPU); the first live run (`20261006T090318Z-7`) was a stale
pre-T-062 server and is listed for reference only.

| run | playing tier @ reported delay | latency_ms.total p50 | vision fps (last) | dropped / frames | enrolled_s at 12 s | isolated at |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| first live run (stale server, pre-T-062) | gate @ **3000 ms** (bug: slowest tier) | 3025 ms | 15-16 | 196 / 296 (66 %) | 1.84 | never |
| before (main, AV tier on MPS) | audio-only/gate @ 13 ms, then focus_hq @ 3000 ms at 10.6 s | 37.7 ms (p90 3024) | 16 | 173 / 299 (58 %) | 1.44 | 10.6 s (AV) |
| after, 1st session after warm | gate @ 13 ms | 36.2 ms | 26 | 13 / 299 (4.3 %) | 0.8 | n/a (12 s) |
| after, 2nd session | gate @ 13 ms | 36.7 ms | 26 | 2 / 299 (0.7 %) | 1.1 | n/a (12 s) |
| after, `--loop` 47 s | gate @ 13 ms → enrolled @ **1500 ms** at 26.8 s | 37.4 ms gate / 1524 ms enrolled | 18 (25 in gate phase) | 219 / 1167 (18.8 %) | 2.0 | **26.8 s** (enrolled) |
| after + ASD GPU wait 20 ms, `--loop` 46 s (\*) | gate @ 13 ms, enrolled @ 1500 ms from 36 s | 37.9 ms | 25 | 187 / 1143 (16.4 %) | 2.0 | 38.6 s |

AV-MossFormer2 on MPS (before): compute p50 621-632 ms, p95 715-1043 ms per 1 s hop. Enrolled
(MossFormer2_SS + ECAPA, balanced 1 s / 0.5 s) on MPS: compute p50 235-253 ms, p95 262-404 ms per
0.5 s hop; MossFormer2_SS separation alone p50 328 ms on a 1 s window (idle machine, 10 calls);
ECAPA embed p50 18 ms. Light-ASD two tracks: MPS p50 19.9 ms, CPU p50 37.1 ms (`python -m
hearing.vision.light_asd`), so moving the ASD to CPU would not fit the 40 ms frame next to MediaPipe.

Per 4 s bucket of the 47 s loop (ASD wait 100 ms): gate phase 0-3 dropped frames per ~97, enrolled
phase 29-37 dropped (17-19 fps, 9-17 ASD skips): each skip held the vision worker 100 ms. With a
20 ms wait (\*) the enrolled-phase buckets dropped 2-12 per ~97 with 39-71 ASD skips. (\*) That run
shared the machine with two other agents' torch benchmarks (load average 7.6), which also explains
its gate-phase drops (7-39 per bucket) and the later enrolled hand-over; re-measure on an idle
machine at the rehearsal.

T-075 attempt on an RTX 4500 Ada). In `auto` the laptop's vision dropped 34 % of frames (other agents' load
on laptop-a), which delayed the lock and the AV tier.

Same-gender association on the A100 with the AV tier (`eval/association_eval.py`, all 10 scenes × 2 variants

same-gender pairs. With the guard on, a same-gender pair gets the AV tier, so the AV row is "guard + AV".

| pair | tier (GPU, A100) | pooled acc / worst | wrong s / 10 s pooled / worst | other played (windows) | locked | GO (acc ≥ 0.95, wrong < 0.5) | Mac T-069 Enrolled `final`, same 20 scenes: acc / wrong |

Guard + AV reaches the bar on both same-gender pairs (M/M 0.970 / 0.47, F/F 0.965 / 0.31); M/M's wrong-target
rate is just under 0.5. The AV tier does not use the voice, so the pair barely matters to it: M/F 0.934 differs
from M/M by 7 of 198 scored windows (M/F does not take the AV-only path: the guard leaves Enrolled on for it,
and T-069 measured M/F Enrolled at 2 s GO). The Enrolled rows on CUDA match the Mac within 0.06 acc and 0.16 s / 10 s
on the same 20 scenes, so the GPU changes nothing for voice matching. Where AV goes wrong: `b_speaks_a_silent`
(target silent all scene, the other talks) plays the other talker for 0.3-2.2 s per 10 s, because the AV model
always outputs something when the target's lips are still (the Enrolled tier is still enrolling there and plays
the mix). The other misses are single mid-scene windows (1-2 per scene); in `a_speaks_b_silent` (other talker
silent) a miss means the output's SI-SDR against the target was ≤ 0 dB, i.e. a poor window, not the wrong person.
AV locks after one window (2 s), 7-8 s on `mouth_no_sound`. An earlier `--fast` run (5 hardest scenes, Enrolled only) gave 0.802 / 0.663 / 0.747 acc;
it is superseded by the 20-scene rows above. Raw JSON: `$HEARING_RESULTS_DIR/t075b/{av,enr}_{mf,mm,ff}.json`
(copied from `~/hearing-remote/eval/results/t075b/` on iLab).

    srun --overlap --jobid=<job> .venv/bin/python -m eval.association_eval --pair mm --candidate av_mossformer2 \
        --vision-cache /tmp/$USER-assoc-vcl-mm --out eval/results/t075b/av_mm.json

## Synthetic/Text ASR feed: caption WER and phrase latency (T-074)

Host laptop-a (M4 Pro, macOS arm64, device `mps`), `python -m eval.synth_feed_bench --demo --av 8`
(`HEARING_FIXTURES_DIR` = the main checkout's `generated/`). Each clip (the demo clip and the first 8
`av_full` two-face fixtures, 3 s of GRID looped to 12 s + 3 s tail) streams in real time through a real
`Session` (MediaPipe + Light-ASD vision, the chosen ASR feed, local faster-whisper ASR, Kokoro TTS,
`speak=true`), the same path `/replay` uses. Caption WER = final captions vs the target's transcript
repeated once per 3 s loop (`eval.metrics.wer`). Phrase latency = ASR phrase end → first TTS sample on
the stream clock. One run per cell, a loaded laptop (one other process at most); n = 9 clips.

| feed | spec in front of `synth` | caption WER (mean, 9 clips) | phrases / 12 s clip | phrase latency p50 (ms) | output silent | ladder tier played |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| mixture | `passthrough` | 1.18 | 3.6 | 1390 | 71 % | n/a |
| natural | `dfn_ll` | 1.22 | 4.2 | 3100 | 62 % | n/a |
| focus-gate | `tiers(only="gate")` | 1.38 | 4.3 | 2490 | 62 % | gate 100 % |
| focus-enrolled | `tiers(only="enrolled,gate")`, oracle enrollment | 1.14 | 4.6 | 2931 | 65 % | gate 87 % (0-100 %), enrolled 13 % |

Demo clip alone: mixture 4 phrases / p50 2060 ms; natural 5 / 3110; focus-gate 5 / 2250; focus-enrolled 5 / 3110
(before this task: 1 phrase, p50 3290 ms, 94 % silent). **Every feed gives ≥ 3 phrases in 12 s**.

Reading the table honestly:
- **WER is not conclusive.** All feeds sit at 1.1-1.4 (> 1 because Whisper small hallucinates filler on
  GRID's 3 s two-talker mixtures and the 6-word reference is short). The feed differences are within
  clip-to-clip noise (per clip 0.83-3.9); no feed is shown better. The offline bake-off remains the SI-SDRi evidence.
- **The Enrolled tier does not sustain on the Mac next to local Whisper + Kokoro.** Live auto-enrollment
  never completes in these clips (they have no 2 s of the target talking alone), so the enrolled row seeds
  the enroller with the clean target / interferer stems (`OracleEnrollment`, a bench-only oracle). Even then
  the tier reports `overrun` most of the time and plays 0-19 % of the 12 s (T-072 saw it play from ~27 s
  in a loop). Without oracle seeding the ladder stays on `gate` for the whole 12 s.
  So in a short demo `focus` plays the gate tier: duck-and-DFN, no overlap separation.
- **Phrase latency target (< 1500 ms) is met only by `mixture`** (1390). Cutting at an energy dip after 1.8 s
  and hard at 3 s (was one 8 s phrase) gives 4-5 phrases per clip, but the median phrase waits 2.2-3.1 s on
  the ladder feeds: ~0.4-0.8 s ASR finalize (whisper re-run on the window), then Kokoro first-sentence
  compute 0.8-1.8 s p50 while the ladder and vision compete for the machine (`tts_p50` in the stats).
- **Segmentation before/after.** Before (8 s max-phrase, no dip cut) is measured only on the demo clip:
  1 phrase, p50 3290 ms. After (dip cut after 1.8 s, hard cut at 3 s): 4-5 phrases per clip, table above.
  The 8-clip set was not re-run with the old 8 s cap (the old cap yields one phrase per 3 s GRID clip, so
  there is no meaningful p50 to compare). The < 1500 ms target is **not met** on natural / focus-gate /
  focus-enrolled (2.2-3.1 s) because ASR finalize plus Kokoro compute under load dominate, not segmentation.
- The session telemetry `tier` shows `av`/`audio-only` on mixture/natural rows: it is the video-presence
  label, not a ladder tier (those chains have no ladder).

## Synthetic/Text default feed back to the mixture (T-079)

Host laptop-a (M4 Pro, macOS arm64), `python -m eval.synth_feed_bench --demo --feeds mixture,auto,natural --modes text,synthetic`
(run 1), then `--feeds mixture,auto --modes synthetic` twice (runs 2, 3). Demo clip `grid-two-face.mp4` looped to 12 s + 3 s
tail, local faster-whisper + Kokoro, real-time through `Session`. **Heavily loaded machine** (load average 14-16, three other
swarm workers' Python processes at 170-400 % CPU), unlike T-074's "one other process at most". One clip, 3-6 phrases per cell,
so a p50 moves by a second between identical runs. `auto` is the new default (no pipeline override): `mixture`, `focus` after
2 s of `context.speakers >= 2`; on this two-talker clip it flipped to focus after 1.7-7.8 s and the flip kept the synth stage
(no phrase or caption dropped, no error). Caption p50 = phrase end -> final caption on the stream clock (the Text-mode number).

| feed | mode | run | caption WER | phrases | phrase latency p50 (ms) | caption p50 (ms) | feed in effect (share of 10 Hz telemetry) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| mixture | text | 1 | 1.46 | (no TTS) | n/a | 10 | n/a (explicit) |
| auto | text | 1 | 1.88 | (no TTS) | n/a | 130 | |
| natural | text | 1 | 1.29 | (no TTS) | n/a | 130 | n/a (explicit) |
| mixture | synthetic | 1 / 2 / 3 | 1.46 / 1.58 / 1.58 | 4 / 3 / 3 | **1290** / 2475 / 2510 | 10 | n/a (explicit) |
| auto | synthetic | 1 / 2 / 3 | 1.62 / 1.38 / 1.46 | 4 / 6 / 5 | 3472 / 2129 / 2213 | 30 / 410 / 270 | mixture 59 % / 13 % / 13 %, then focus |
| natural | synthetic | 1 | 1.33 | 5 | 3110 | 130 | n/a (explicit) |

T-074 reference on the demo clip: mixture p50 2060 ms, natural 3110 ms (9-clip mixture p50 1390 ms). The one-talker default chain
is now exactly T-074's mixture row (`passthrough>synth`), measured 1290-2510 ms here under load; natural reproduced at 3110 ms.
On the two-talker demo clip `auto` spends most of the run on `focus` (the gate tier plays) and pays the ladder's cost,
2129-3472 ms. WER is inconclusive as in T-074 (all > 1, Whisper filler on 3 s GRID loops).

## ASD freshness during enrolled windows (T-083)

Host laptop-a (M4 Pro, macOS arm64, Python 3.12, MPS). `create_app()` on an ephemeral port, then the 12 s
`grid-two-face.mp4` replayed `--loop` for 55 s in `focus_hq` at suppression 100 (the ladder starts at
gate and reaches the enrolled tier); numbers are `telemetry.vision` (`asd_age_ms` = p50/p95 of the gap
between fresh `p_speaking` updates over the last 250 updates, ms; read from the last telemetry of each
tier). The machine was shared with other agents. "Before" = `HEARING_ASD_CPU=0` (GPU busy for 20 ms ->
skip frame, hold `p_speaking`); the CPU fallback runs Light-ASD on a CPU copy when the GPU lock is busy,
at most once per `ASD_CPU_GAP_S`.

| run | tier | ASD age p50 / p95 | ASD skipped / CPU | vision fps | dropped / frames |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| before | gate | 40 / 388 | 430 / 0 | 22 | 262 / 1342 |
| before | enrolled | 40 / **318** | 390 / 0 | 22 | 240 / 1262 |
| CPU every frame | enrolled | 45 / 189 | 0 / 178 | **11** | 475 / 1199 |
| CPU gap 120 ms | enrolled | 41 / 228 | 315 / 98 | 20 | 241 / 1373 |
| **CPU gap 80 ms (shipped)** | gate | 41 / 179 | 191 / 107 | 20 | 265 / 1298 |
| **CPU gap 80 ms (shipped)** | enrolled | 42 / **188** | 212 / 119 | 19 | 285 / 1373 |

The p95 during enrolled windows drops 318 -> 188 ms (< 200 ms) for about 3 fps of vision. A fair
lock alone cannot do it: the enrolled window holds the GPU 240-330 ms, longer than the 200 ms target.
Single runs on a loaded machine; the audio-only seconds at start-up (model load) show ages above 700 ms in both.

## Enrolled tier under CPU load: adaptive hop (T-089, 2026-10-06)
laptop-a (M4 Pro, MPS), `hearing serve` + `scripts/replay.py --loop --mode focus_hq` on
`eval/fixtures/demo/grid-two-face.mp4` for 82 s, telemetry at 10 Hz; enrolled tier 1 s window /
0.5 s base hop, threaded. The machine was never idle (other swarm workers): "ambient" is no extra
load, "4 burners" adds 4 busy-loop shells. Server = mean processing per 20 ms client frame over
each 100 ms telemetry interval.

| run | load avg (start -> end) | time to isolated | enrolled share after | hops used | enrolled compute p50 / p95 per window | utilisation (p50/hop) | server p50 / p95 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| main 0087fc8, before (T-089 brief) | ~9.5 | 30 s | 0 % (overrun for 50 s) | 0.5 | not logged | > 1 | not logged |
| 4 burners, before the cold-window fix | 13.7 -> 16.7 | 36.4 s | 65 % (2 drops to gate on overrun at hop 1.0) | 0.5, 1.0 | 747 / 860-1640 ms | 0.71-0.90 | 8.4 / 13.9 ms |
| **4 burners (shipped)** | 11.9 -> 17.0 | **30.4 s** | **100 %** (72.5 s telemetry, 0 drops) | 0.5 -> 0.75 at 33 s -> 0.5 at 70 s | 312-455 / 388-603 ms | 0.42-0.61 at 0.75 s | 6.1 / **9.8 ms** |
| ambient (shipped) | 17.0 -> 45.5 | 30.1 s | 100 % (76 s telemetry, 0 drops) | 0.5 | 236-309 / 250-443 ms | 0.47-0.62 | 4.2 / 6.5 ms |
| `scripts/demo.sh --check` (shipped) | 39 | n/a | n/a | 0.5 | 214 / 220 ms (noise, 6 windows) | 0.43 (headroom 57 %, pass) | n/a |

Time to isolated is dominated by auto-enrollment (2 s of the target talking alone, reached at
26-28 s on this clip). Enrolled compute per window under ambient load settles at 236-267 ms p50,
the bake-off's 236 ms idle figure: T-078 (shared enroller) added no measurable cost. Before the fix
the first computed window after enrollment took 7.9 s (MPS kernels + enrollment embeddings), and its
overruns pushed the hop straight to 1.0 s, where hop = window leaves no overlap; that window is now
"cold": it neither widens the hop nor enters the compute stats.

## Suppression slider sweep (T-093)
Two runs in parallel on laptop-a (Apple M4 Pro, DNSMOS P.835 ONNX via `eval/dnsmos.py`, git `1099d17`, 2026-10-06), **16 clips per set**:
`.venv/bin/python -m eval.slider_sweep --n 16 --no-focus` (natural, `slider_sweep_20261006-160829.json`) and
`.venv/bin/python -m eval.slider_sweep --n 16 --no-natural` (focus_hq, `slider_sweep_20261006-160728.json`), both in `$HEARING_RESULTS_DIR`.
Sets: `tiny` 000-015 (one talker in babble, SNR -5..10 dB) and `av_full` two-face 008-023 (target + other talker + babble).
natural = `dfn_ll>gate>mixer` streamed in 10 ms chunks, no camera (the gate bypasses, the slider acts through DFN's `atten_lim_db`);
focus_hq = the Mac ladder's enrolled tier `tse(enrolled, 1.0 s / 0.5 s)` + the mixer (`est + room_gain(s)·(mix - est)`), enrolled on the clip's own clean target (no second utterance in the set).
Room dB = output minus its projection on the clean target, re the mixture (0 at slider 0); it includes the other talker and babble speech.
Means over the 16 clips:

| mode | set | slider | curve dB | room dB | SI-SDRi | SIG | BAK | OVRL |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| natural | tiny (1 talker + babble) | 0 / 25 / 50 / 75 / 100 | 0 / -7.5 / -15 / -22.5 / -inf | 0 / -3.9 / -5.6 / -6.2 / -6.5 | -0.19 / 0.34 / 0.04 / -0.40 / -0.92 | 2.23 / 2.62 / 2.82 / 2.87 / **2.85** | 1.59 / 1.93 / 2.22 / 2.42 / 2.47 | 1.52 / 1.82 / 2.04 / 2.15 / 2.15 |
| natural | two-face | same | same | 0 / -5.1 / -8.2 / -9.4 / -10.1 | 0.02 / 1.94 / 2.99 / 3.16 / 2.73 | 1.31 / 1.60 / 2.13 / 2.28 / **2.14** | 1.20 / 1.50 / 2.10 / 2.56 / 3.00 | 1.13 / 1.31 / 1.66 / 1.85 / 1.82 |
| focus_hq (enrolled + mixer) | two-face | same | same | 0 / -6.1 / -10.0 / -11.5 / -12.0 | 0.00 / 5.24 / 8.47 / 9.61 / 9.55 | 1.26 / 1.40 / 2.26 / 2.79 / **2.92** | 1.18 / 1.26 / 1.81 / 2.49 / 3.40 | 1.11 / 1.17 / 1.60 / 2.05 / 2.41 |

**SIG at 100 vs 50** (mean): natural tiny +0.03, natural two-face +0.01, focus_hq +0.66: within 0.3 everywhere, so DFN's
strength is **not capped**: BAK keeps rising to 100 while SIG holds, i.e. no musical-noise trade at the top. Per clip, 6 of 32
natural clips and 1 of 16 focus_hq clips lose more than 0.3 SIG from 50 to 100 (worst -0.95, a -5 dB SNR babble clip).
The room level follows the curve for the first 25 points and then flattens at -6 to -12 dB: what is left is speech
(babble, the other talker) that DFN keeps by design and the TSE estimate leaks. The slider asks for the curve; the models bound it.

Demo clip in Natural at each slider (`eval/fixtures/demo/grid-two-face.mp4`, 12 s each, a real `hearing serve` on an ephemeral port

## Natural mid-slider quality check (T-099, 2026-10-06)
Question: does `dfn_ll>gate>mixer` comb-filter at a partial slider (room blended back out of time with the 13 ms
processed path)? **No.** In this chain the mixer passes through (no residual side channel); the room is blended back
*inside* DFN as `atten_lim_db` on the noisy spectrum of the **same STFT frame** the mask and deep filter act on, so both
summands carry the same algorithmic delay. `tests/test_slider_alignment.py`: cross-correlation lag between the output at
slider 0 (room only) and slider 100 (processed only) is **0 samples** with the mock model and with the real
`DeepFilterNet3_ll` weights (tone in noise, and impulses); a partial slider equals the linear blend
`m·out(0) + (1-m)·out(100)` to 1e-4 with no spectral notches. No code change was needed.

Re-measured (laptop-a M4 Pro, git `ff37a9a`+tests, load avg 5.8 at start, run under `swarm-lock --exclusive`):
`.venv/bin/python -m eval.slider_sweep --n 8 --no-focus` (`slider_sweep_20261006-163837.json`), means over 8 clips, slider 0 = the input:

| set | slider 0 (input) | 25 | 50 | 75 | 100 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| tiny (1 talker + babble), DNSMOS OVRL | 1.55 | 1.82 | 2.02 | 2.10 | 2.07 |
| tiny, SIG / BAK | 2.29 / 1.61 | 2.64 / 1.92 | 2.85 / 2.17 | 2.90 / 2.35 | 2.85 / 2.36 |
| two-face (target + talker + babble), DNSMOS OVRL | 1.12 | 1.19 | 1.31 | 1.44 | 1.56 |
| two-face, SIG / BAK | 1.28 / 1.21 | 1.40 / 1.32 | 1.68 / 1.53 | 1.80 / 1.79 | 1.89 / 2.07 |

OVRL never falls below the input at any slider and rises monotonically to 75 on both sets (tiny 75 -> 100 is -0.03, inside
clip-to-clip noise).

**Live replay of the demo clip (T-099 attempt 2).** Each slider is its own `hearing serve` session fed by `hearing.replay`
(12 s, no camera lock, `HEARING_PIPELINES=natural=<chain>`), scored with `scripts/session_score.py` (real DNSMOS); the
s = 0 session (DFN bypass: the room through the 48 kHz resample + STFT, 13 ms late) is the input baseline. Script:
`.venv/bin/python .swarm-run/sweep_live.py "<chains>" 0,25,50,75,100` (a loop over `scripts/demo_regress.py`'s
server/replay helpers), laptop-a M4 Pro, git `cecc0b5`+tests, under `swarm-lock --exclusive`, load avg 4.6 at start:

| clip `demo-mf-easy`, chain | DNSMOS OVRL s=0 (input) | 25 | 50 | 75 | 100 | SI-SDRi dB at 0/25/50/75/100 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `dfn_ll>gate>mixer` | 1.43 | 2.21 | 2.21 | 2.49 | 2.93 | 0.0 / +2.9 / +2.6 / +2.0 / +1.1 |
| `dfn_ll` (server default) | 1.43 | 2.11 | 2.59 | 2.64 | 3.03 | 0.0 / +0.2 / 0.0 / -0.4 / -0.8 |

Monotonic and above the input at every partial slider on both chains: no mid-slider collapse on the current demo clip.

| clip `grid-two-face.mp4` (legacy 3 s SIR-0 loop, the task's clip), chain | s=0 (input) | 25 | 50 | 75 | 100 | true dropouts s at 0/25/50/75/100 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `dfn_ll>gate>mixer` | 1.22 | 1.34 | 1.82 | 1.64 | 1.64 | 0 / 0 / 0.16 / 1.94 / 2.78 |
| `dfn_ll` (server default) | 1.22 | 1.24 | 1.27 | 1.25 | 1.62 | 0 / 0 / 0 / 0 / 0.08 |

This reproduces the task's numbers (`dfn_ll` 1.27 at 50, 1.62 at 100), but its baseline was wrong. The s = 0 session on this
clip scores **1.22**, not 1.56, so 25-75 is level with the room or slightly above it (+0.02 to +0.05), not below. Why it is
flat: half of this clip's interference is a second GRID talker at SIR 0 dB. DFN keeps speech, so a 7.5-22 dB attenuation limit
removes only the babble and DNSMOS barely moves until the limit is lifted at 100. `dfn_ll>gate>mixer` is better at 50 (1.82)
because the gate ducks the room between the target's words. At 75-100 it scores lower than at 50 because the gate (no camera
lock in a replay) cuts 1.9-2.8 s of the target (true dropouts). That belongs to T-096's gate; `gate.py` was not changed here.
The third ablation chain (`dfn_ll>gate`) was not run (not measured: run time limit). In this chain the mixer passes through, so
it would have matched `dfn_ll>gate>mixer`.

**Where the "below the input" came from.** The raw input is not the right baseline. Offline, `tests/test_slider_alignment.py`
(tiny speech + babble at 5 dB SNR, real `dfn_ll>gate>mixer`) scores raw input 1.51 but s = 0 (bypass) 1.28; s = 25/50/75/100
then score 2.29 / 2.48 / 2.54 / 2.81. Bypass costs 0.23 OVRL without any processing: prepending 13 ms of zeros to the input
alone takes it from 1.51 to 1.32 (DNSMOS on a short clip moves with where its 9 s segments cut it), and the resampler takes
10.5 dB off the 7-8 kHz band (0.0 dB below 6 kHz) for the rest. So comparing live outputs against an input scored straight
from the MP4 makes every output look about 0.3 worse. T-093's s = 0 session on `grid-two-face.mp4` scored 1.24, while the
input was quoted as 1.56. Partial sliders beat the s = 0 session on every clip measured here. The 1.23 that `demo_regress`
recorded for natural on `grid-two-face.mp4` (`demo_regress_20261006-125934`, s = 80, default chain `dfn_ll`) is level
with T-093's s = 0 of 1.24 on that clip (the legacy 3 s SIR-0 loop: half of the "noise" is a second talker DFN keeps).
`tests/test_slider_alignment.py::test_real_dfn_ll_partial_slider_never_below_the_room` (slow) pins it: OVRL at s = 25/50/75/100
>= s = 0, and never more than 0.1 lower than the slider below.

## Live replay (what the judge hears) (T-084, 2026-10-06)

### Default clip `demo-mf-easy` (T-097, new baseline)

`eval/fixtures/demo/demo-mf-easy/` (`python -m eval.make_fixtures --demo`, `eval/demo_scene.py`): a 30 s two-face GRID scene in the demo's shape, target `grid-s1` (M) on the left, other `grid-s4` (F) on the right; 0-5.4 s target alone (sentences with 0.32 s pauses), 5.4-15.3 s alternating turns, 15.3-24.4 s overlap at SIR 0 dB, 24.4-30 s target alone; six-talker babble (the committed 3 s clip, tiled) at SNR 10 dB to the target's speech level. Stems (target, other, noise) and the per-face speaking timeline are in its manifest. Siblings: `demo-mf-hard` (SNR 5), `demo-mm` (s1/s2), `demo-ff` (s4/s7). Replay, `demo_regress.sh` and the console's REPLAY button default to it; its regression baseline only counts runs on the same clip.

Run `demo_regress_20261006-121310` (`bash scripts/demo_regress.sh --modes natural,focus_hq --seconds 30`) on laptop-a (Apple M4 Pro, MPS, Mac ladder, no remote GPU, load average ≈ 11 from other swarm workers), wall 76 s:

| mode | suppression | target SI-SDRi dB | other-talker leak dB | true dropouts s | intended ducking s | DNSMOS OVRL | delay ms (reported total) | other-dominant s | first isolated | tier switches | target switches |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| natural | 80 | **-0.23** | **-9.1** | **0.00** | 3.06 | **2.92** | **13** (36) | 4.4 | never | 1 | 0 |
| focus_hq | 100 | **-4.48** | **-12.1** | **8.48** | 3.98 | **2.68** | **13** (42) | 2.6 | never | 3 | 1 |

Input: SI-SDR +1.95 dB against the target stem (the legacy loop's was -0.95 dB). What it says: with 5 s of the target alone at the start the input is cleaner, but neither mode reaches Isolated within 30 s on the Mac; focus_hq's true dropouts are the gate at suppression 100 ducking the target while it talks (its SI-SDRi is below natural's for the same reason). These are the numbers to beat; the tables below are the **legacy loop** (`grid-two-face.mp4`, `make_fixtures --demo-legacy`).

### `demo-mf-easy` with the manifest target locked (T-100, current baseline)

The run above let TargetSelector choose, and on this clip both faces are off-centre: it picked the right face (the other talker) most of the time, so the engine isolated the wrong person and the scorer, comparing against the intended target, read it as a bad separation (`demo_regress_20261006-124326`: focus_hq SI-SDRi -10.8 dB). `demo_regress.sh` now locks the manifest's target for an ingested clip by default (the `scripts/replay.py --lock-target` oracle: the face whose box centre is within 0.15 of the manifest box), which is what the demo recipe does in the room: **the wearer faces the target**. `--no-lock` replays every mode free (its own baseline variant, `...:free`). Every row now carries `target agreement` = share of targeted telemetry ticks whose target track's box centre is on the manifest target's side of the frame; below 80 % the score line and the table say **WRONG TARGET**. One extra `free-selection` row (focus_hq, no lock) tracks what the selector does on its own; it is reported, never flagged.

Run `demo_regress_20261006-124938` (`bash scripts/demo_regress.sh --modes natural,focus_hq --seconds 30` under `swarm-lock --exclusive`) on laptop-a (Apple M4 Pro, MPS, Mac ladder, no remote GPU, load average 5.2 before / 6.6 after), git `T-100` on `e27e4ba`, wall 100 s. This is the baseline for variant `full:natural,focus_hq:30:lock` (the regress compares against the median of the last 5 runs of the same variant on the same clip):

| row | mode | suppression | target SI-SDRi dB | other-talker leak dB | true dropouts s | intended ducking s | DNSMOS OVRL | delay ms (reported total) | other-dominant s | first isolated s | tier switches | target switches | target agreement |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| locked | natural | 80 | **+0.06** | **-8.3** | **0.00** | 2.78 | **2.71** | **13** (35) | 4.6 | never | 1 | 0 | 100 % |
| locked | focus_hq | 100 | **+3.71** | **-19.7** | **0.64** | 3.58 | **2.95** | **1500** (1524) | 0.4 | 7.3 | 4 | 0 | 100 % |
| free-selection | focus_hq | 100 | **-3.56** | **-13.3** | **5.12** | 3.96 | **3.11** | **13** (37) | 1.6 | 7.3 | 4 | 1 | 38 % **WRONG TARGET** |

Input: SI-SDR +1.95 dB (natural) / +1.69 dB (focus_hq window) against the target stem. What it says: with the target locked, Focus HQ reaches Isolated at 7.3 s and gives +3.7 dB SI-SDRi with the other talker 19.7 dB down (T-090's manual `--lock-target` replay measured +3.0 dB / -17.3 dB / 0.84 s dropouts on the same clip); left to itself the selector sits on the wrong face for 62 % of targeted ticks. Free selection on a scene where neither face is centred is not a product bug to chase before the demo: the recipe has the wearer face the target (a centred face wins the selector's centre prior), and `target agreement` makes a wrong-target run impossible to misread as a separation regression.

### Natural = `dfn_ll>gate>mixer` by slider (T-102)

The server's default Natural pipeline in T-102 only; since T-103 it is the `natural_gate` option and the default is plain `dfn_ll` (next table). Runs `demo_regress_20261006-174558`, `-174812` and `-175026` (`swarm-lock --exclusive -- .venv/bin/python scripts/demo_regress.py --modes natural@25,natural@50,natural@75,natural@100 --seconds 30 --no-free`, git `8999b3f` = T-102 on main with T-096's gate and T-100's lock) on laptop-a (Apple M4 Pro, MPS, no remote GPU, load average 4.3-9.3), 30 s of `demo-mf-easy` per position, **manifest target locked** (target agreement 100 % in every row). Three runs; one value where all three agree, else run 1 / 2 / 3:

| slider | target SI-SDRi dB | other-talker leak dB | true dropouts s | intended ducking s | DNSMOS OVRL | delay ms (reported total) | tier switches |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 25 | +1.88 / +2.11 / +2.26 | -8.1 | 0.00 | 3.64 | 2.20 / 2.21 / 2.20 | 13 (35) | 1 |
| **50 (judge default)** | **+2.41 / +2.39 / +2.26** | **-11.7 / -11.7 / -11.3** | **0.00** | 3.88 / 3.88 / 3.82 | **2.31 / 2.30 / 2.30** | 13 (35) | 1 |
| 75 | +2.13 | -13.4 | 0.06 (1/14 onsets clipped) | 4.00 | 2.48 | 13 (35) | 1 |
| 100 | +1.68 / +1.64 / +1.68 | -14.8 / -14.7 / -14.8 | 0.12 (1/14 onsets clipped) | 4.06 | 2.77 | 13 (35) | 1 |

Input: SI-SDR +1.95 dB, DNSMOS OVRL **1.55** (SIG 2.61, BAK 1.33; the input mixture scored on the same aligned samples by `score_arrays(x, x)`). What it says: s=50 has the best SI-SDRi of the four, no true dropouts, the other talker 11.7 dB down, and lifts DNSMOS by **+0.75** over the input, so it is the judge's Natural default (`JUDGE_NATURAL_SUPPRESSION`). s=75-100 push the other talker further down and score higher DNSMOS (more of the noisy room is removed) but start to clip a word onset. An earlier set of runs without the lock and before T-096's gate (`demo_regress_20261006-132522`/`-132736`/`-133653`, git `2030efe`) scored SI-SDRi -3 to -5 dB with 6-7 s true dropouts at s>=75: the selector sat on the wrong face, so that was the wrong-target case T-100 describes, not the gate. With no target at all the gate bypasses (`tests/test_gate.py::test_no_target_holds_unity_gain_at_any_slider`). Against the locked T-100 natural row (plain `dfn_ll` at s=80: SI-SDRi +0.06, leak -8.3, DNSMOS 2.71), the gate chain at s=50 gains +2.3 dB SI-SDRi and 3.4 dB of leak.

### Natural default: plain `dfn_ll` s=80 vs `natural_gate` s=50 (T-103)

Clip `demo-mf-easy` (`eval/fixtures/demo/demo-mf-easy/clip.mp4`), 30 s per run, laptop-a (Apple M4 Pro, MPS, no remote GPU), git `b794c34`, every run under `swarm-lock --exclusive`: `HEARING_PIPELINES="natural=<chain>" .venv/bin/python scripts/demo_regress.py --modes natural@<s> --seconds 30 --no-free --clip <clip>` (add `--no-lock` for free selection). Three runs per row; one value where all three agree, else run 1 / 2 / 3. Input SI-SDR +1.95 dB.

|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **`dfn_ll` (default)** | **80** | manifest lock | **+0.06** | **-8.3** | 0.00 | **2.71** | 100 % | 13 (35) | `demo_regress_20261006-185618`, `-185732`, `-185846` | 6.4 / 5.3 / 6.3 |
| **`dfn_ll` (default)** | **80** | free (selector) | **+0.06** | **-8.3** | 0.00 | **2.71** | 0 / 0 / 2 % | 13 (35) | `-190341`, `-190459`, `-190614` | 6.4 / 22.4 / 10.5 |
| `natural_gate` = `dfn_ll>gate>mixer` | 50 | manifest lock | +2.16 / +2.42 / +2.41 | -11.7 | 0.00 | 2.31 | 100 % | 13 (35) | `-185656`, `-185810`, `-185923` | 7.3 / 6.7 / 5.9 |

What it says: plain `dfn_ll` gives the same output whatever the camera does (it has no target), so its row cannot go wrong on stage. The gate chain is +2.3 dB SI-SDRi and 3.4 dB less leak **only when the right face is locked**; when the selector sits on the other face (free selection on this clip, T-100) it ducks the real target and drops to -2.6 to -5.9 dB SI-SDRi, matching Q-240's -3.06 dB / DNSMOS 2.12. DNSMOS favours plain `dfn_ll` even with the lock (2.71 vs 2.31). So Natural defaults to `dfn_ll` at s=80 (`JUDGE_NATURAL_SUPPRESSION`) and the other talker is Focus HQ's job; `natural_gate` stays one name away (`HEARING_PIPELINES="natural=natural_gate"`) for re-evaluation when gate improvements land. Free-selection run 3 of the gate chain was flagged by `demo_regress` and replayed once (-2.33 dB, 37 % agreement); both are listed.

The earlier remote run `demo_regress_20261006-190208` (before T-108) scored -7.6 dB because telemetry reported the local fallback ladder's 1500 ms while the remote played at 1700 ms; rescored with the open-lag check it is **+5.5 / +3.9 dB** (`session_score.py ... -v`).

### Focus HQ through the iLab A100 on current code (T-110, 2026-10-07)

Why the first T-086-era GPU run (`demo_regress_20261006-210125`, git `85ec7e5`) reported **1723 ms** and leaked **-10.3 dB**: the remote server was still the one started at T-075 time (`/health` `git_rev: unknown`, the rsynced tree has no `.git`), playing `~/hearing-remote/config/tiers-remote.yaml` with enrolled at **1.0 s / 0.5 s** (1.0 + 0.5 + 0.2 s buffer = 1.7 s), not T-086's 0.5 s / 0.125 s. `gpu_up.sh` now writes the synced commit to `~/hearing-remote/REVISION` and `/health` reports it; the server was restarted on current code **inside the same SLURM job 432518** (`ILAB_REUSE=432518 REMOTE_PORT=18768 ILAB_NODE=rlab2 ILAB_GRES=gpu:a100:1 scripts/gpu_up.sh ilab`: an `srun --jobid --overlap` step; `/health` `git_rev 682f5d3…`).

Two more fixes came out of the live runs. (1) The remote enrolled tier had a plain `mixer`: a window that missed the target played a hole (1.14 / 1.42 s true dropouts, `210811`); it now has the Mac's `mixer(max_duck_db=20)` (T-095). (2) The job has **2 CPUs** (`AllocTRES cpu=2`) and the remote session (vision + enroller + tse in one Python process) runs at 100 % of a core, so enrolled compute is **p50 147-162 ms per window** live (69 ms offline in T-086). The stage widens its hop 0.125 → 0.25 s (hence 950 ms, not 825), still overruns now and then, and the remote ladder (no overrun tolerance) then dropped to AV (3.2 s) and back: `211207` scored +0.5 / +2.0 dB, leak -7.3 / -11.6 dB. `config/tiers-remote.yaml` now has the Mac ladder's policy (`downgrade_after_s: 10`, `min_switch_s: 10`, `cooldown_s: 0`): enrolled keeps playing through an overrun. The laptop also forwards `control.isolation` and `control.profile` to the remote session now, so the judge profile's strict isolation and auto-calibration run on the GPU ladder too (`demo_regress` sends neither, so the rows below are standard isolation on both backends, like the Mac rows).

Final: three runs `demo_regress_20261006-211552`, `-211703`, `-211815` (`HEARING_REMOTE_URL=ws://127.0.0.1:18765/ws/session swarm-lock --exclusive -- bash scripts/demo_regress.sh --modes focus_hq --no-free --clip …/demo-mf-easy`, 60 s, manifest target locked, git `682f5d3`) on laptop-a (Apple M4 Pro, load average 5.1-6.2), remote on the A100 (rlab2, tunnel probe RTT p50 45 ms right after start). "Enrolled phase" = `scripts/session_score.py --enrolled-phase` (only `enrolled` ticks). Mac rows: the three locked 60 s Mac-ladder runs on the same clip from T-095 (`192539`, `192748`, `194700`, load 4.5-7.7), rescored the same way.

Phase DNSMOS OVRL (same sessions; input `in.wav` 1.74 on this scorer): **A** before Enrolled 1.83-1.84, Enrolled 2.59-2.80 (Isolated at 13.4-14.3 s); **J** 1.46 / 2.64; **D** 1.67-1.90 / 2.67-2.82 (Enrolled at 23.8-27.9 s: the AV tier played in between); **G** 2.14-2.17 / 2.47-2.78 (Isolated at **7.0-8.9 s**); **DG** 2.08 / 2.87 (run 1).

What it says. (1) **The GPU is not worse once Enrolled plays.** Enrolled-phase DNSMOS is 2.6-2.8 on every variant (the Mac's whole-run 2.66-2.71, T-110's Mac rows), and enrolled-phase leak reaches -15 to -18 dB in the good runs. The whole-run gap is the first ~14 s: the remote ladder plays the room (`passthrough`) or the plain gate there (DNSMOS 1.5-1.8), and `min_switch_s: 10` held the gate → enrolled promotion until ~10 s after the gate came in. **G** (`min_switch_s: 3`, now the default in `config/tiers-remote.yaml`) brings Isolated to 7.0-8.9 s, whole-run leak -10.7 / -13.6 / -15.0 dB (A: -5.3 to -9.5), SI-SDRi +5.1 / +5.1 / +5.9 dB (A: +0.1 to +5.2), DNSMOS 2.50 / 2.66 / 2.58 (A: 2.49-2.59), true dropouts 0.06 / 0.06 / 0.00 s, delay 950 ms, no tier switch after Enrolled except one late demotion to the gate in run 220353. (2) **Isolation/profile forwarding works** (J: the session runs `profile: judge`, `isolation: strict`, and `remote._forward_control` sends both to the GPU session, T-110; the remote's own telemetry is not in the laptop's session tap, so the remote side was not read back), but strict isolation alone does not close the gap: J scores like A (+2.0 to +3.5 dB, leak -6.8 to -10.1, DNSMOS 2.42-2.50; it ducks pauses, which lowers DNSMOS), because the same 14 s of room come first. (3) **The DFN post-filter (T-086's `enrolled_dfn`) does not fit live**: a CPU DeepFilterNet3 on the remote session pushes the enrolled tier into overrun (233-465 overrun ticks per minute vs 0-105 without it, compute p50 150-250 ms per window), so the ladder spends long stretches on the AV tier (3200 ms delay in 2 of 7 sessions) and true dropouts rise to 0.06-0.30 s. Its DNSMOS is the highest (2.59-2.85 whole run, 2.62-2.95 enrolled phase), so it remains the option once the remote has CPU to spare. (4) **0.5 / 0.125 s is not affordable live** on this job: every session widened the enrolled hop to 0.25 s (enrolled compute p50 125-208 ms per 0.5 s window, against 69 ms offline in T-086); the delay is 950 ms (983 ms with DFN), not 825 ms. Asking SLURM for more CPUs is refused (`srun --cpus-per-task`: "Job rejected: Please do not specify the number of CPUs. There is actually no limit", so `ILAB_CPUS` cannot be used on iLab). (5) **DNSMOS ≥ 2.9 is not reached** by any shippable variant: the limits are the room-quality first ~7 s (no DFN tier on the GPU box, T-075) and the enrolled overruns on the remote CPU, which forbid the post-filter. The Mac's "3.1-3.2" was not reproduced on this clip by any Mac run in this task or in T-110 (Mac judge-profile replay here: 2.79 whole run, 2.82 enrolled phase).

### Legacy loop (`grid-two-face.mp4`)

The 12 s demo clip (`eval/fixtures/demo/grid-two-face.mp4` = `av_full/012_two_face_snr5_sir0_babble`, target = left face `grid-s1`, other talker = right face `grid-s4`, babble at 5 dB SNR, 0 dB SIR, the 3 s mixture looped) replayed through a real `hearing serve` on a free port, recorded with `HEARING_SESSION_LOG`, and scored against the clip's clean stems by `scripts/session_score.py`. Output is aligned piecewise to the input by the per-tick stream delay from telemetry (`latency_ms.algorithmic + resample`, or `tiers.delay_ms`), refined by cross-correlation; the input is aligned to the stems loop by loop (the MP4's AAC loop drifts up to ~50 ms per loop). Leak = energy of the other talker's least-squares projection in the output over the same in the input (200 ms frames). True dropouts = target active in its clean stem (energy VAD, -35 dB) while the output is > 25 dB below it, runs ≥ 60 ms; intended ducking (target silent, other talking, output ≥ 6 dB down) is counted apart. DNSMOS P.835 by `eval/dnsmos.py` (ONNX models). `delay` = measured stream delay; `reported total` = telemetry `latency_ms.total` (adds server compute and the 20 ms capture frame, which are not in the recorded stream).

```
bash scripts/demo_regress.sh          # natural, focus_hq, text, synthetic; 60 s each since T-095 (was 24 s); ~5 min
bash scripts/demo_regress.sh --fast   # natural + focus_hq, 12 s each (run by verify_full.sh)
.venv/bin/python scripts/session_score.py results/sessions/<dir> [-v]   # any recorded session
```

### Focus HQ over 60 s on the Mac: ladder stability (T-095, 2026-10-06)

`bash scripts/demo_regress.sh --modes focus_hq --seconds 60` on laptop-a (Apple M4 Pro, MPS, Mac ladder enrolled → gate → audio-only, no remote GPU), load average 8-21 from other worktrees the whole time (no window with load < 4 came up, so the unloaded run is not measured). Scored with the T-095 scorer: a tier segment is a run of **output** samples at one delay and is mapped back to input `[start - d, end - d)` (before, it was cut at the same times on the input, so the last 1.5 s of every enrolled run was scored against the gate's audio; `tests/test_session_score.py::test_alignment_is_piecewise_across_tier_switches`). "Enrolled phase" = the same session scored from 0.5 s after the ladder first plays `enrolled` (`score_session(skip_s=...)`). After merging main's T-104 scorer (`session_score.py --enrolled-phase`: only the `enrolled` ticks, each run aligned on its own, input cropped with the same mask) on top of the T-095 output-time segments, the overall scores are unchanged (`182209` +6.41, `182058` +4.74 dB) and `--enrolled-phase` gives +11.42 / +9.71 dB against +11.29 / +9.77 from the skip-based column below.

| run (`eval/results/demo_regress_…`) | ladder | SI-SDRi dB | true dropouts s | tier switches | first isolated s | enrolled phase SI-SDRi dB | leak dB | DNSMOS OVRL | load |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 20261006-120122 (task report, old scorer) | main `ea01f44` | -3.55 | 12.12 | 7 | 30.9 | - | -10.4 | 2.13 | 9-10 |
| same recording, T-095 scorer | main `ea01f44` | -3.00 | 9.96 | 7 | 30.9 | - | -10.2 | - | 9-10 |

What it says. (1) The −3.5 dB was mostly real; the scorer artefact was 0.5-1.9 dB and ~2 s of dropouts. (2) The flapping came from the enrolled tier overrunning under load (health `overrun` for ~2 s at a time, every 2.5-4 s) and the ladder dropping to the gate at the first overrun; enrolled itself is +6 to +11 dB whenever it plays. Holding it through overruns (a skipped window falls back to the mix for that hop) is worth far more than any switch. (3) The **gate**, not enrolled, was the tier that made Focus HQ worse than the mixture: the target's ASD reads p ≥ 0.5 for ~51 % of its speech on this overlapped clip, and at suppression 100 the gate ducked the rest to silence (true dropouts 6-10 s per 30 s of gate); `dfn_ll` at full strength alone costs 2 dB SI-SDR here (-0.96 → -2.97 dB, offline on the recorded input) while cutting the other talker's leak by ~10 dB. The final bridge tier gives up that leak reduction (leak -3 to -4 dB over the run) for no holes. (4) The enrolled-phase dropouts of attempt 1 (0.8 / 1.4 / 3.4 s) were windows where the enrolled TSE missed the target and the mixer at slider 100 played nothing else; the enrolled tier's mixer now keeps the residual at ≥ −20 dB (`mixer(max_duck_db=20)`), the same bound as the gate, so a miss plays the target 20 dB down instead of a hole. Offline on the attempt-1 recordings that removed every enrolled-phase dropout at no SI-SDR cost, and the three shipped runs confirm it live: true dropouts 0.08 s each (the dip of the gate → enrolled switch), SI-SDRi +2.1 to +2.9 dB, +10.3 to +10.7 dB once enrolled plays. Enrolled overran for 2.6-5.0 s in total in each shipped run (telemetry ticks), in streaks shorter than 1.5 s, so the 1.5 s grace run played the same as the 10 s one; the 10 s grace stays because the load-21 run had 20 s of overruns. The "never worse than the gate" guard was removed (its ASD energy proxy favoured the gate by construction). (5) On main's new default clip (`demo-mf-easy`, T-097, target locked by T-100) the shipped ladder gives **+4.5 to +5.0 dB** over the minute (main's only earlier `full:focus_hq:60:lock` run: +3.07 dB, 0.92 s dropouts), **+9.4 to +11.5 dB** once enrolled plays, **0.00 s** true dropouts, 0/27 onset clips, 2 switches, target agreement 100 %, Isolated at 18.3 s. (6) After merging main's T-089 (threaded enrolled windows, hop widened on overrun before the tier goes unhealthy) and T-092 (enrollment): 4 runs, 5 replays. Every replay where the target lock held met the targets: **+4.7 / +6.4 / +3.0 / +5.4 dB**, **+9.8 / +11.3 / +4.7 / +11.1 dB** once enrolled plays, true dropouts **0.00-0.22 s**, **2** switches, **Isolated at 13.4-14.4 s** (≤ 15 s, met now). The +3.0 / +4.7 dB replay ran at load ~20, where T-089 widened the enrolled hop to 1 s (delay 2.0 s); it stayed on enrolled with no switches and no holes. The one failure (replay 1 of `182320`, load 5.5 → 20, -1.4 dB) is a **target lock loss**: vision delivered no frames for ~0.4 s at 33.0 s, the lock dropped and the selector picked the other face. Every ladder move after that was a hard demotion (enrollment is reset on a target switch, the gate needs a face), not flapping. demo_regress's confirm replay of the same run scored +3.0 dB. The run with load < 4 was not taken: the closest was load 4.1-5.7 (`182058`). (7) After merging main's T-102 and T-107 (git `72da0c1`), three more replays at load 4.5-7.7: **+5.05 / +5.42 / +4.55 dB**, **+9.65 / +9.60 / +9.67 dB** in the enrolled phase (main's `--enrolled-phase` scorer), true dropouts **0.00 / 0.00 / 0.20 s**, **2** switches each, target agreement 100 %, Isolated at **14.4 / 14.8 / 15.5 s** (the third is 0.5 s over the 15 s target: its enrolled hop widened once, delay 1750 ms). (8) After merging main's T-103 and T-108 (git `af4b7bb`; the scorer keeps this task's input-time segments and adds T-108's open 0-4 s lag check, which raised no alignment note on any of the runs above: rescored 192539 / 192748 / 192900 unchanged), one more replay at load 5.3-5.5: **+6.14 dB**, **+11.6 dB** in the enrolled phase, **0.00 s** dropouts, **2** switches, Isolated at **13.9 s**.

Run `demo_regress_20261006-112003` on laptop-a (Apple M4 Pro, MPS, Mac ladder, no remote GPU), git `d906706` + T-084, wall 131 s, 24 s of clip per mode:

| mode | suppression | target SI-SDRi dB | other-talker leak dB | true dropouts s | DNSMOS OVRL | delay ms (reported total) | other-dominant s | first isolated | tier |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| natural | 80 | **-1.36** | **-9.4** | **0.00** | **1.53** | **13** (36) | 3.0 | never | av (dfn_ll>gate) |
| focus_hq | 100 | **-0.49** | **-11.5** | **5.60** | **1.53** | **13** (38) | 1.0 | never | gate from 1.8 s (target acquiring, never locked) |

| session | mode | target SI-SDRi dB | leak dB | true dropouts s | DNSMOS OVRL | delay ms (reported total) | first isolated s | tier switches |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 20261006T150935Z-4 | natural | -4.05 | -8.2 | 0.00 | 2.10 | 13 (36) | never | 1 |
| 20261006T150936Z-5 | focus_hq | -0.14 | -12.2 | 16.28 | 2.56 | 13 (38); 1500 in enrolled windows | 34.4 | 9 (audio-only → enrolled ↔ gate) |
| 20261006T090318Z-7 | focus_hq | -2.62 | -12.2 | 1.78 | 1.76 | 3000 (3025) | never | 1 |
| 20261006T091222Z-9 | natural | -0.36 | -9.4 | 0.00 | 1.55 | 13 (35) | never | 3 |

What it says: on this clip neither mode isolates the left talker yet. Natural ducks the room a little (leak -9 dB) but takes the target down with it (SI-SDRi below 0, other talker dominant in 3 of 24 s). Focus HQ on the Mac moved audio-only → gate at 1.8 s but never locked the target inside 24 s, so no TSE tier ran ("first isolated" never reached); its true dropouts are the gate at suppression 100 ducking the target to silence while it talks; in the 76 s run it reached the enrolled tier after 34 s and then switched enrolled ↔ gate 9 times with 16 s of true dropouts. Text plays no audio by design (its "dropouts" are the whole clip). Regression rule in `demo_regress.sh`: against the per-mode median of the last 5 runs of the same variant, an SI-SDRi drop > max(1 dB, those runs' spread) or > max(0.5 s, spread) more true dropouts flags the mode; a flagged mode is replayed once more and only a regression both replays show exits 1.

Run-to-run noise (why the rule is not a plain 0.5 s): seven 12 s focus_hq replays of the same code (git `673d243`, laptop-a, 2026-10-06) gave true dropouts 1.20 / 2.78 / 2.70 / 2.28 / 2.44 / 2.44 / 1.32 s and SI-SDRi -0.06 to +0.50 dB; natural was identical every run (-0.36 dB, 0.00 s). The focus_hq spread comes from the gate's ASD timing while the target is still being acquired. A fixed 0.5 s rule against the single previous run flagged a regression on unchanged code (1.20 → 2.78 s).

Real clip (`python -m eval.synth_feed_bench --demo --feeds mixture --modes synthetic --asr local --tts local`, faster-whisper + Kokoro fp32, `swarm-lock --exclusive`, load 4-9 before/after; the two-face grid demo clip, 3 phrases): `synth_phrase_p50` **1450 ms** (phrase end = pause 0.4 s + Whisper compute + TTS). The criterion 'first word < 400 ms after final ASR' is met for the TTS part in isolation (3-word head 242 ms fp32) and for the mock-ASR stage bench (TTS part 428 ms, close to the limit), but is **not met end to end with real Whisper**: Whisper's compute per phrase (not separated in this bench) and the 0.4 s pause dominate. The 'before' on the real clip was not re-measured (int8 build gone). With 7-word phrases the queue backs up (playout is real time), so that bench is not a first-word measure.

### Gate-only Focus HQ before/after (T-096, 2026-10-06)

`HEARING_PIPELINES='focus_hq=tiers(only="gate,audio-only")' bash scripts/demo_regress.sh --modes focus_hq,natural --seconds 30`, three runs each, laptop-a (Apple M4 Pro, MPS), same clip as above (28.4 s scored per run). "Before" = git `ea01f44` (gate attack/release/hold 20/250/150 ms, on/off 0.5/0.35, duck to `1 - s` = digital silence at suppression 100); "after" = T-096 gate (30/300/400 ms, on/off 0.3/0.2, both-talking partial gain from the ASD margin down to -12 dB, lip-confidence partial duck between p 0.05 and 0.2 up to -6 dB, -30 dB duck floor, stale-ASD hold at 200 ms). Input: SI-SDR -0.96 dB, DNSMOS OVRL 1.56. Onset clips = target onsets after >= 200 ms of target silence whose first 60 ms of output were silent (new `session_score` field). Before run 3 was flagged and replayed; the table keeps the better replay (-1.41 dB / 5.16 s; the first one was -2.3 dB / 6.38 s).
